## Comprehensive Extraction Pipeline Analysis

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's what I found about how projects and features are classified:

---

## 1. LLM PROMPTS AND CLASSIFICATION RULES

The primary extraction prompt is in **`/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/prompt.ts`** and defines these specific classification rules:

### Entity Types (Line 5):
```
"Entity kinds: project, feature, task, decision, question."
```

### Feature Classification (Line 33):
```
"- Feature: capability/requirement or scoped artifact description 
  (supports, provides, enables, needs to handle, must support; 
  cards/dashboards/flows/screens/integrations)."
```

### Project Classification (Implied):
Projects are treated as **named commitments** for building something, not explicitly detailed in the same way as features. The examples show:
- **Line 69**: "Input: I'm building Schack Systems and shipping the first release in April. => project: Schack Systems; decision: shipping the first release in April."
- **Line 67-68**: Placeholder rejection - "My project needs work" extracts nothing (generic reference).

### Key Distinguishing Factors:

**For Feature extraction**, the prompt emphasizes:
- Capability/requirement language ("supports", "provides", "enables", "needs to handle", "must support")
- Scoped artifact descriptions (cards, dashboards, flows, screens, integrations)
- Prioritization/scoping statements without explicit execution action

**For Project extraction**, the prompt implies:
- Named products or systems being built
- Concrete named concepts, not generic placeholders
- The prompt filters out vague references: "my project, the project, our tool, the thing, this idea, that thing, my app, it, this, that" (Line 50)

---

## 2. ZEPHYR SCHEMA DEFINITIONS

**Validation schema in `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/schema.ts`:**

```typescript
const extractionEntityBaseSchema = z.object({
  tempId: z.string().min(1),
  kind: z.enum(["project", "feature", "task", "decision", "question"]),
  text: z.string().min(3).max(200),
  confidence: z.number().min(0).max(1),
  evidence: z.string().min(1),
}).strict();
```

All entities require:
- **kind**: Must be one of the 5 types
- **text**: Extracted entity text (3-200 characters)
- **confidence**: Numeric score 0-1
- **evidence**: Verbatim snippet from source text
- **tempId**: Temporary ID for relationships during extraction

---

## 3. OVERALL ENTITY TYPE SYSTEM

**Complete EntityKind enum (shared contracts):**

From `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/shared/contracts.ts`:
```typescript
export type EntityKind = "workspace" | "project" | "person" | "feature" | "task" | "decision" | "question";
```

**Breakdown:**
- **workspace**: System-wide container (not extracted, only created during workspace setup)
- **project**: Named business initiative or product
- **feature**: Capability, requirement, or scoped artifact
- **task**: Concrete executable work (action verbs: implement, build, fix, migrate, deploy, set up, write, test, review, handle)
- **decision**: Commitment/selection language (let's go with, move forward with, we decided, I'm choosing, going with, settled on, committed to, approved)
- **question**: Explicit question framing or question mark
- **person**: Referenced in text but NEVER persisted as extracted entity (only as attribution fields on other entity kinds)

**Type refinements:**
- `ExtractableEntityKind = Exclude<EntityKind, "workspace">` (5 types actually extracted)
- `PersistableExtractableEntityKind = Exclude<ExtractableEntityKind, "person">` (4 types persisted to database)

---

## 4. EXTRACTION PIPELINE FLOW

**Flow: `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/extract-graph.ts`**

The LLM extraction:
1. Takes conversation history (for reference resolution only)
2. Takes current message (extract only from this text when message metadata exists)
3. Takes existing graph context (semantic index of prior extracted entities)
4. Calls `generateObject()` with `extractionResultSchema` and system prompt
5. Returns `ExtractionPromptOutput` with entities, relationships, tools

**Persistence: `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/persist-extraction.ts`**

1. Post-validates entities against confidence thresholds (store >= 0.6, display >= 0.85)
2. Validates evidence is grounded in source text
3. For each entity:
   - Loads workspace candidates of the same kind using `loadWorkspaceKindCandidates()`
   - Checks for exact name match (normalized)
   - Falls back to embedding similarity (cosine > 0.95 with fuzzy name match = merge)
   - Creates or merges entity with provenance edge
4. Handles person attribution separately (links to existing person records or stores assignee names)
5. Creates embedding for entities
6. Persists relationships between entities

---

## 5. DEDUPLICATION AND MERGING

**From `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/dedup.ts`:**

Entities are merged based on similarity:
- **> 0.95 similarity + fuzzy name match** = Auto-merge
- **0.8-0.95 similarity** = Possible duplicate (flagged but not auto-merged)
- **< 0.8 similarity** = Independent entities

The system prefers "richer" names (longer, more detailed) when merging:
```typescript
export function isRicherEntityName(incomingName: string, existingName: string): boolean {
  // Returns true if incoming name is longer and has more words
}
```

---

```

**Multi-Entity Example:**

=> Extract:

   - feature "auth feature"
   - decision "use SurrealDB"

## 7. DATABASE SCHEMA FOR PROJECTS VS FEATURES

**Project table** (`/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/schema/surreal-schema.surql`, lines 56-64):
```sql
DEFINE TABLE project SCHEMAFULL;
DEFINE FIELD name ON project TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD status ON project TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD description ON project TYPE option<string>;
DEFINE FIELD embedding ON project TYPE option<array<float>>;
DEFINE FIELD created_at ON project TYPE datetime;
DEFINE FIELD updated_at ON project TYPE option<datetime>;
DEFINE INDEX idx_project_embedding ON project FIELDS embedding HNSW DIMENSION 1536 DIST COSINE;
```

**Feature table** (lines 66-77):
```sql
DEFINE TABLE feature SCHEMAFULL;
DEFINE FIELD name ON feature TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD status ON feature TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD description ON feature TYPE option<string>;
DEFINE FIELD prd ON feature TYPE option<string>;
DEFINE FIELD owner ON feature TYPE option<record<person>>;
DEFINE FIELD owner_name ON feature TYPE option<string>;
DEFINE FIELD embedding ON feature TYPE option<array<float>>;
DEFINE FIELD created_at ON feature TYPE datetime;
DEFINE FIELD updated_at ON feature TYPE option<datetime>;
DEFINE INDEX idx_feature_embedding ON feature FIELDS embedding HNSW DIMENSION 1536 DIST COSINE;
```

**Key differences:**
- **Feature** has owner/owner_name fields (person attribution)
- **Feature** has prd (product requirements document) field
- **Project** is simpler (no ownership or PRD)

---

## 8. PROMPT-GUIDED EXTRACTION EXAMPLE

The system prompt includes specific examples (lines 59-71):

```
"Examples:
- Input: I decided to use event sourcing for audit logs. => decision text: use event sourcing (no person entity).
- Input: Let's move forward with schema validation first. => one decision text: schema validation (not task + decision).
- Input: Our stack includes SurrealDB and Bun. => entities: none; tools: SurrealDB, Bun.
- Input: Existing PM tools like Linear and Notion are not what we are building. => entities: none; tools: none.
- Input: We considered Redis but chose Memcached for caching. => decision text includes Memcached; tools include Memcached only.
- Input: Yes, let's go with that. Context has assistant recommendation with details and earlier user mention of SurrealDB. => decision text: SurrealDB; tools include SurrealDB; do not include assistant-only rationale phrases; set resolvedFromMessageId to user message id that introduced SurrealDB.
- Input: Ok let's go with the first option for the graph. Context options are SurrealDB and Postgres. => decision text: SurrealDB; tools include SurrealDB; resolvedFromMessageId points to message with SurrealDB option.
- Input: My project needs work. => entities: none; tools: none.
- Input: The feature is broken. => entities: none; tools: none.
- Input: I'm building Schack Systems and shipping the first release in April. => project: Schack Systems; decision: shipping the first release in April.
- Input: The platform will ingest chat, extract decisions and tasks, store them in SurrealDB, and render component cards for high-confidence entities. => task: extract decisions and tasks; decision: store them in SurrealDB; avoid extra entities for ingest/render clause fragments.
- Input: Deduplicate features by embedding similarity and keep the more descriptive name when merged. => feature: Deduplicate features by embedding similarity (single entity)."
```

---

## 9. KEY VALIDATION THRESHOLDS

From `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/validation.ts`:

```typescript
shouldStoreExtraction(confidence >= 0.6)      // Persists to database
shouldDisplayExtraction(confidence >= 0.85)   // Shows to user in UI
hasGroundedEvidence(evidence, sourceText)     // Evidence must be verbatim snippet
```

---

## 10. PERSON ATTRIBUTION HANDLING

**From `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/person.ts`:**

Only these entity kinds can have person attribution:
- **feature** → owner/owner_name
- **task** → owner/owner_name
- **decision** → decided_by/decided_by_name
- **question** → assigned_to/assigned_to_name

**Projects do NOT support person attribution.**

Person entities are never persisted. Instead, `assignee_name` from extraction is converted to:
- A lookup against existing workspace people (exact name match)
- Or stored as `*_name` text field if person not found

---

## Summary

The extraction system distinguishes **projects** from **features** through:

1. **Linguistic markers**: Projects are named commitments ("I'm building X"); features describe capabilities ("supports X", "enables Y")
2. **Schema structure**: Features have owner/PRD fields; projects don't
3. **Placeholder rejection**: Both "my project" and "the feature" are rejected as vague
4. **Named specificity**: "Schack Systems" (project) vs "real-time dependency conflict detection" (feature)
5. **Scope**: Features are scoped artifacts (cards, dashboards, integrations); projects are named products/initiatives
6. **Confidence-based persistence**: Both types require >= 0.6 confidence to store, >= 0.85 to display

The five extractable entity types form a hierarchy: **project → (contains) → feature → (implements) → task/decision/question**

## Summary: Projects and Features Usage in Zagreb-V3

I've completed a thorough exploration of how projects and features are used across the UI, graph queries, chat workflows, and business logic. Here's what I found:

### 1. **Frontend UI Rendering (Component Layer)**

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/client/chat-component-catalog.tsx`

Projects and features are displayed through two main UI components:
- **EntityCard**: Renders individual extracted entities with kind, name, status, and confidence
- **ExtractionSummary**: Groups multiple entities in a grid layout with relationship count

Both components support all entity types including "project" and "feature" via the `kindLabelByKind` mapping:
```typescript
const kindLabelByKind: Record<ExtractableKind, string> = {
  project: "Project",
  feature: "Feature",
  // ... others
};
```

The EntityCard displays:
- Kind label (e.g., "Project" or "Feature")
- Entity name and status
- Confidence score (0.00-1.00 format)

### 2. **Graph Relationships & Schema**

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/schema/surreal-schema.surql`

The data model defines these project/feature relationships:

**Tables:**
- `project`: name, status, description, embedding, timestamps
- `feature`: name, status, description, prd, owner record, embedding, timestamps

**Relation Tables (TYPE RELATION):**
- `has_project`: `workspace -> project` (creates edges between workspace and its projects)
- `has_feature`: `project -> feature` (creates edges between project and its features)
- `has_task`: `feature -> task` (creates edges from features to their tasks)
- `belongs_to`: `(task|decision|question) -> (feature|project)` (entities belong to containers)
- `touched_by`: `project -> conversation` (tracks which conversations mention which projects)

**Key Graph Traversals:**
- `workspace:id -> has_project -> project:id` (list all projects in workspace)
- `project:id -> has_feature -> feature:id` (list all features in project)
- `feature:id -> has_task -> task:id` (list all tasks in feature)

### 3. **Graph Queries (SurrealQL Implementation)**

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/graph/queries.ts`

Key exported types and functions for project/feature:
```typescript
export type SearchEntityKind = "project" | "feature" | "task" | "decision" | "question";
export type ProjectStatus = {
  project: { id: string; name: string };
  tasks: { active, completed, blocked, recent },
  decisions: Array<{ id, name, status }>,
  questions: Array<{ id, name, status }>,
  features: Array<{ id, name, status }>
};
```

**getProjectStatus()** function (lines 969-1075):
Queries a project and returns:
1. Features: `SELECT id, name, status FROM feature WHERE id IN (SELECT VALUE out FROM has_feature WHERE in = $project)`
2. Tasks: Both direct project tasks AND tasks nested under features
3. Decisions, questions, and feature list

This demonstrates a hierarchical query pattern: `project -> has_feature -> feature -> has_task -> task`

### 4. **Chat Tools and Workflows**

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/chat/tools/`

Available tools that reference projects/features:

1. **search_entities** (`search-entities.ts`):
   - Filters by entity kinds including "project" and "feature"
   - Optional project scoping: `project: z.string().optional()`
   - Returns ranked results with neighbors

2. **get_project_status** (`get-project-status.ts`):
   - Takes projectId as input
   - Returns complete project overview including features, tasks, decisions, questions

3. **get_entity_detail** (`get-entity-detail.ts`):
   - Works with all entity types including projects and features
   - Returns relationships and provenance

**Context Loading** (`context.ts`):
Builds chat context including:
```typescript
workspaceSummary: {
  projects: WorkspaceProjectSummary[],  // with active task counts
  recentDecisions: WorkspaceDecisionSummary[],
  openQuestions: WorkspaceQuestionSummary[]
}
```

### 5. **Extraction & Entity Persistence**

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/`

**Schema** (`schema.ts`):
- Projects and features are extractable entities with confidence scores and evidence
- Can have optional assignee names and resolvedFromMessageId

**Project/Feature Association Logic** (`entity-upsert.ts`):
- Features are automatically linked to projects via `ensureProjectFeatureEdge()`
- Uses `resolveEntityProject()` to infer which project a feature belongs to

**Extraction Prompt** (`prompt.ts`):
```
Entity kinds: project, feature, task, decision, question.
- Prioritization/scoping statements without explicit execution action are feature.
- Input: I'm building Schack Systems and shipping the first release in April. 
  => project: Schack Systems; decision: shipping the first release in April.
```

### 6. **Workspace Scope Management**

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/workspace/workspace-scope.ts`

Core functions:
- **ensureWorkspaceProjectEdge()**: Creates/maintains `workspace -> has_project -> project` edges
- **ensureProjectFeatureEdge()**: Creates/maintains `project -> has_feature -> feature` edges
- **loadWorkspaceProjects()**: Queries all projects in a workspace
- **resolveEntityProject()**: Heuristically determines which project an extracted entity belongs to by:
  1. If only 1 project, use it
  2. If multiple projects, match entity text against project names in prompt
  3. Return only if exactly 1 match (avoid ambiguity)

### 7. **Conversation Sidebar & Project Grouping**

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/workspace/conversation-sidebar.ts`

Sidebar organization uses project-centric grouping:

- **groupConversationsByProject()**: Groups conversations by dominant project using strict majority heuristic
  - A conversation is assigned to a project only if that project's entity count > 50% of total
  
- **resolveEntityToProjects()**: Maps entities to projects:
  - `project -> [project]` (self)
  - `feature -> has_feature -> project` (parent project)
  - `task/decision/question -> belongs_to -> (feature|project)` (direct or via feature)

- **loadProjectFeatureActivity()**: Retrieves top 5 features per project by latest extraction activity:
  ```sql
  SELECT out.id AS featureId, out.name AS featureName, 
         math::max(extracted_at) AS latestActivityAt
  FROM extraction_relation
  WHERE out IN (SELECT VALUE out FROM has_feature WHERE `in` = $project)
  GROUP BY out
  ORDER BY latestActivityAt DESC
  LIMIT 5
  ```

### 8. **Onboarding Workflow**

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/onboarding/onboarding-state.ts`

Onboarding completion requires:
- At least 1 project (`projectCount >= 1`)
- At least 1 person (`personCount >= 1`)
- At least 1 decision or question
- OR at least 7 conversation turns

### 9. **Component Rendering**

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/components.ts`

Extractions are rendered as components in markdown format:
```json
{
  "type": "EntityCard",
  "props": {
    "kind": "feature",
    "name": "Mobile app redesign",
    "confidence": 0.95,
    "status": "captured"
  }
}
```

---

## Key Architectural Patterns

1. **Hierarchical Graph Model**: `Workspace -> Project -> Feature -> Task` forms a container hierarchy
2. **Project-as-Anchor**: Projects are the primary organizational unit for conversations and context
3. **Bidirectional Traversal**: Both `extraction_relation` (what was extracted) and `touched_by` (what touched what) are maintained for different query patterns
4. **Confidence-Based Filtering**: All extracted entities have confidence scores; UI and system prompt respect threshold filtering
5. **Entity Deduplication**: Features are deduplicated by embedding similarity with fuzzy name matching
6. **Lazy Project Resolution**: Project assignment for extracted entities happens during persistence using heuristic matching

## Summary Report: Project and Feature Entities

Based on my thorough exploration of the Zagreb-v3 codebase, here's a comprehensive analysis of how projects and features are defined, differentiated, and used:

---

## 1. SCHEMA DEFINITIONS

### Project Table (`schema/surreal-schema.surql`, lines 56-64)
```surql
DEFINE TABLE project SCHEMAFULL;
DEFINE FIELD name ON project TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD status ON project TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD description ON project TYPE option<string>;
DEFINE FIELD embedding ON project TYPE option<array<float>>;
DEFINE FIELD created_at ON project TYPE datetime;
DEFINE FIELD updated_at ON project TYPE option<datetime>;
DEFINE INDEX project_name ON project FIELDS name;
DEFINE INDEX idx_project_embedding ON project FIELDS embedding HNSW DIMENSION 1536 DIST COSINE;
```

**Fields:**
- `name` (string, required) - The project name/identifier
- `status` (string, required) - Current status (e.g., "active")
- `description` (optional string) - Project description
- `embedding` (optional float array) - 1536-dimensional embedding for semantic search
- `created_at` / `updated_at` - Temporal tracking

### Feature Table (`schema/surreal-schema.surql`, lines 66-77)
```surql
DEFINE TABLE feature SCHEMAFULL;
DEFINE FIELD name ON feature TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD status ON feature TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD description ON feature TYPE option<string>;
DEFINE FIELD prd ON feature TYPE option<string>;
DEFINE FIELD owner ON feature TYPE option<record<person>>;
DEFINE FIELD owner_name ON feature TYPE option<string>;
DEFINE FIELD embedding ON feature TYPE option<array<float>>;
DEFINE FIELD created_at ON feature TYPE datetime;
DEFINE FIELD updated_at ON feature TYPE option<datetime>;
DEFINE INDEX feature_name ON feature FIELDS name;
DEFINE INDEX idx_feature_embedding ON feature FIELDS embedding HNSW DIMENSION 1536 DIST COSINE;
```

**Fields:**
- `name` (string, required) - The feature name
- `status` (string, required) - Current status
- `description` (optional string) - Feature description
- `prd` (optional string) - Product requirements document reference
- `owner` / `owner_name` (optional) - Person assigned to the feature
- `embedding` (optional float array) - 1536-dimensional semantic embedding
- `created_at` / `updated_at` - Temporal tracking

**Key Difference:** Features have `prd`, `owner`, and `owner_name` fields that projects lack, indicating features are more scoped and assignable units of work within projects.

---

## 2. RELATIONSHIPS BETWEEN PROJECTS AND FEATURES

### Direct Relationship: `has_feature` Edge (`schema/surreal-schema.surql`, lines 202-203)
```surql
DEFINE TABLE has_feature TYPE RELATION IN project OUT feature SCHEMAFULL;
DEFINE FIELD added_at ON has_feature TYPE datetime;
```
- **Direction:** `project --has_feature--> feature`
- **Semantics:** A project HAS multiple features; features belong to exactly one project
- **Established:** In `workspace-scope.ts` via `ensureProjectFeatureEdge()` when a feature is extracted and a parent project is resolved

### Hierarchical Hierarchy: `has_project` Edge (`schema/surreal-schema.surql`, lines 205-206)
```surql
DEFINE TABLE has_project TYPE RELATION IN workspace OUT project SCHEMAFULL;
DEFINE FIELD added_at ON has_project TYPE datetime;
```
- **Direction:** `workspace --has_project--> project`
- **Semantics:** Workspaces contain projects, projects contain features, features contain tasks

### Secondary Relationships to Other Entities

**Tasks belong to features via `has_task`** (`schema/surreal-schema.surql`, lines 208-209):
```surql
DEFINE TABLE has_task TYPE RELATION IN feature OUT task SCHEMAFULL;
DEFINE FIELD added_at ON has_task TYPE datetime;
```

**Tasks, decisions, questions can belong to projects via `belongs_to`** (`schema/surreal-schema.surql`, lines 256-257):
```surql
DEFINE TABLE belongs_to TYPE RELATION IN task | decision | question OUT feature | project SCHEMAFULL;
DEFINE FIELD added_at ON belongs_to TYPE datetime;
```
- Tasks/decisions/questions can link to EITHER a feature OR a project directly when their parent project cannot be automatically resolved

---

## 3. EXTRACTION & CLASSIFICATION LOGIC

### Entity Kind Enumeration
From `/app/src/shared/contracts.ts`, line 1:
```typescript
export type EntityKind = "workspace" | "project" | "person" | "feature" | "task" | "decision" | "question";
```

### Extraction Schema
From `/app/src/server/extraction/schema.ts`, line 5:
```typescript
kind: z.enum(["project", "feature", "task", "decision", "question"]),
```
- Projects and features are both first-class extractable entity kinds
- **Note:** `workspace` and `person` are not extracted (workspace is system-level, person uses assignee_name instead)

### Classification Rules from Extraction Prompt

From `/app/src/server/extraction/prompt.ts`, lines 30-36:

**Decision:**
- Commitment/selection language: "let's go with", "move forward with", "we decided", "I'm choosing", "going with", "settled on", "committed to", "approved"

**Task:**
- Concrete executable work with action verbs: "implement", "build", "fix", "migrate", "deploy", "set up", "write", "test", "review", "handle"

**Feature:**
- **Capability/requirement or scoped artifact description**
- Language: "supports", "provides", "enables", "needs to handle", "must support"
- **Artifact examples:** "cards/dashboards/flows/screens/integrations"

**Question:**
- Explicit question framing or question mark

**Critical Rule (line 36):**
- "Prioritization/scoping statements without explicit execution action are feature."

### Project Classification
**Implicit:** Projects are entities with concrete names representing business initiatives. The example (line 69):
```
"Input: I'm building Schack Systems and shipping the first release in April. 
=> project: Schack Systems; decision: shipping the first release in April."
```

Projects are NOT explicitly called out in the classification rules but are inferred from:
- Named business/product initiatives  
- Initiatives mentioned in conversation context as scope containers

---

## 4. EXTRACTION PERSISTENCE & RELATIONSHIP CREATION

From `/app/src/server/extraction/entity-upsert.ts`:

### When a Project is Created (lines 142-148)
```typescript
if (input.extracted.kind === "project") {
  await ensureWorkspaceProjectEdge(input.surreal, input.workspaceRecord, entityRecord as RecordId<"project", string>, input.now);
  input.workspaceProjects.push({
    id: entityRecord as RecordId<"project", string>,
    name: input.extracted.text,
  });
}
```
- **Action:** Automatically creates a `has_project` edge linking the workspace to the new project
- **Side effect:** Project is added to the workspace's project scope list (for resolving future features/tasks)

### When a Feature is Created (lines 150-155)
```typescript
if (input.extracted.kind === "feature") {
  const projectRecord = resolveEntityProject(input.extracted.text, input.promptText, input.workspaceProjects);
  if (projectRecord) {
    await ensureProjectFeatureEdge(input.surreal, projectRecord, entityRecord as RecordId<"feature", string>, input.now);
  }
}
```
- **Action:** Attempts to resolve which project the feature belongs to
- **Resolution logic:** See below
- **Action on success:** Creates a `has_feature` edge linking the project to the feature

### When Tasks/Decisions/Questions are Created (lines 157-164)
```typescript
if (input.extracted.kind === "task" || input.extracted.kind === "decision" || input.extracted.kind === "question") {
  const projectRecord = resolveEntityProject(input.extracted.text, input.promptText, input.workspaceProjects);
  if (projectRecord) {
    await input.surreal.relate(entityRecord, new RecordId("belongs_to", randomUUID()), projectRecord, {
      added_at: input.now,
    }).output("after");
  }
}
```
- **Action:** Creates a `belongs_to` edge linking the entity to a resolved project (if found)

### Project Resolution Logic
From `/app/src/server/workspace/workspace-scope.ts`, lines 155-176:

```typescript
export function resolveEntityProject(
  entityText: string,
  promptText: string,
  projects: ProjectScopeRow[],
): RecordId<"project", string> | undefined {
  if (projects.length === 0) {
    return undefined;
  }

  if (projects.length === 1) {
    return projects[0].id;  // Auto-assign to only project
  }

  const haystack = `${promptText}\n${entityText}`.toLowerCase();
  const matchingProjects = projects.filter((project) => 
    haystack.includes(project.name.toLowerCase())
  );

  if (matchingProjects.length !== 1) {
    return undefined;  // Ambiguous or no match
  }

  return matchingProjects[0].id;
}
```

**Resolution strategy:**
1. If workspace has 0 projects → return `undefined` (no parent)
2. If workspace has 1 project → auto-assign to it
3. If workspace has multiple projects → search for exact project name match in the entity text + full prompt
   - If exactly 1 match found → assign to it
   - If 0 or >1 matches → return `undefined` (unresolved)

---

## 5. ENTITY TEXT EXTRACTION

From `/app/src/server/extraction/entity-text.ts`:

```typescript
if (table === "project") {
  const row = await surreal.select<{ name: string }>(record as RecordId<"project", string>);
  return row?.name;
}

if (table === "feature") {
  const row = await surreal.select<{ name: string }>(record as RecordId<"feature", string>);
  return row?.name;
}
```

Both projects and features read their display text from the `name` field (unlike tasks which use `title`, decisions which use `summary`, questions which use `text`).

---

## 6. ENTITY RECORD CREATION CONTENT

From `/app/src/server/extraction/entity-upsert.ts`, `buildEntityRecordContent()`:

### Project Record Content (lines 262-269)
```typescript
if (kind === "project") {
  return {
    name: text,
    status: "active",
    ...(embedding ? { embedding } : {}),
    created_at: now,
    updated_at: now,
  };
}
```
- **Default status:** "active"
- **Includes:** name, embedding (if available), timestamps
- **No owner assignment** (projects are not assigned to people)

### Feature Record Content (lines 272-279)
```typescript
if (kind === "feature") {
  return {
    name: text,
    status: "active",
    ...(embedding ? { embedding } : {}),
    created_at: now,
    updated_at: now,
  };
}
```
- **Default status:** "active"
- **Includes:** name, embedding (if available), timestamps
- **Note:** `prd`, `owner`, `owner_name` are NOT set at extraction time; they're set later via assignee references or manual updates
- Unlike tasks/decisions/questions, features do NOT store `extraction_confidence` or `source_message` in their records

---

## 7. CANDIDATE ENTITY DEDUPLICATION

From `/app/src/server/extraction/entity-upsert.ts`, `loadWorkspaceKindCandidates()`:

### For Projects (lines 325-333)
```typescript
if (kind === "project") {
  const [rows] = await surreal.query<[CandidateEntityRow[]]>(
    "SELECT id, name AS text, embedding FROM project WHERE id IN (SELECT VALUE out FROM has_project WHERE `in` = $workspace);",
    { workspace: workspaceRecord },
  )
  .collect<[CandidateEntityRow[]]>();
  return rows;
}
```
- Queries all projects in the workspace via `has_project` edges
- Uses exact name match or embedding-based fuzzy matching to deduplicate
- Merges incoming extraction with highest-confidence existing project

### For Features (lines 345-361)
```typescript
if (kind === "feature") {
  const [rows] = await surreal.query<[CandidateEntityRow[]]>(
    [
      "SELECT id, name AS text, embedding",
      "FROM feature",
      "WHERE id IN (",
      "  SELECT VALUE out",
      "  FROM has_feature",
      "  WHERE `in` IN (SELECT VALUE out FROM has_project WHERE `in` = $workspace)",
      ");",
    ].join(" "),
    { workspace: workspaceRecord },
  )
  .collect<[CandidateEntityRow[]]>();
  return rows;
}
```
- Queries all features in all projects in the workspace (via `has_feature` edges)
- Uses same deduplication strategy as projects
- Ensures features are workspace-scoped through their project parent

---

## 8. QUERY PATTERNS IN GRAPH

### Loading Workspace Projects
From `/app/src/server/workspace/workspace-scope.ts`, lines 139-153:
```typescript
export async function loadWorkspaceProjects(
  surreal: Surreal,
  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>,
): Promise<ProjectScopeRow[]> {
  const [rows] = await surreal.query<[ProjectScopeRow[]]>(
    "SELECT id, name FROM project WHERE id IN (SELECT VALUE out FROM has_project WHERE `in` = $workspace);",
    { workspace: workspaceRecord },
  )
  .collect<[ProjectScopeRow[]]>();
  return rows;
}
```

### Getting Project Status
From `/app/src/server/graph/queries.ts`, lines 985-1086 (`getProjectStatus`):

**Features in a project:**
```typescript
const [featureRows] = await input.surreal.query<[Array<{ id, name, status }>]>(
  "SELECT id, name, status FROM feature WHERE id IN (SELECT VALUE out FROM has_feature WHERE `in` = $project);",
  { project: projectRecord },
)
```

**Tasks in a project (direct or via features):**
```typescript
"FROM task WHERE id IN (SELECT VALUE `in` FROM belongs_to WHERE out = $project)"
"OR id IN ("
"  SELECT VALUE out FROM has_task"
"  WHERE `in` IN (SELECT VALUE out FROM has_feature WHERE `in` = $project)"
")"
```

This demonstrates the hierarchy:
- `project --has_feature--> feature --has_task--> task`
- OR direct: `project --belongs_to<-- task`

---

## 9. SEMANTIC DIFFERENCES: PROJECT vs FEATURE

| Aspect | Project | Feature |
|--------|---------|---------|
| **Definition** | Named business initiative or product area | Capability/requirement scoped to a project |
| **Typical Examples** | "Zagreb Platform", "Mobile App", "Analytics Engine" | "User authentication", "Dashboard UI", "Export PDF" |
| **Owner** | None (organizational scope) | Optional person assignment |
| **Status Field** | Yes (simple status) | Yes (simple status) |
| **Additional Metadata** | Only description | PRD, owner, owner_name |
| **Hierarchy** | Workspace → Project | Workspace → Project → Feature |
| **Contains** | Features, tasks, decisions, questions | Tasks (via has_task) |
| **Extraction Language** | Named initiatives or business contexts | "supports X", "provides Y", "enables Z", or artifact names (cards, dashboards, flows, screens) |
| **Relationship Rule** | Auto-link to workspace on creation | Must resolve parent project; link via has_feature |
| **Dedup Scope** | All projects in workspace | All features in all workspace projects |

---

## 10. VALIDATION & DEDUPLICATION

From `/app/src/server/extraction/entity-upsert.ts`, deduplication uses a three-step strategy:

1. **Exact Name Match** (lines 37-65): Normalized name comparison
   - Confidence threshold: ≥0 (always merge)
   - Creates `extraction_relation` provenance edge
   - Returns existing record (no new entity created)

2. **Fuzzy Embedding + Name Match** (lines 71-115): Cosine similarity + fuzzy string matching
   - Cosine similarity threshold: >0.95
   - Fuzzy name match required (Levenshtein-style)
   - Merges with existing record if both conditions met

3. **Possible Duplicate Detection** (lines 166-177): Semantic similarity detection
   - Cosine similarity range: 0.8 to 0.95 (not high enough for merge)
   - Creates `entity_relation` edge with kind `POSSIBLE_DUPLICATE`
   - Allows manual review/consolidation

---

## 11. EXTRACTION DISPLAY & COMPONENTS

From `/app/src/server/extraction/components.ts`:

Both projects and features are displayed in extraction summary cards:
- Sorted by confidence (highest first)
- Limited to top 6 entities per message
- Displayed as `EntityCard` or `ExtractionSummary` component
- Filtered by display threshold (default 0.85 confidence)

---

## KEY INSIGHTS

1. **Projects are workspace-scoped organizational containers** – they auto-link to the workspace on extraction
2. **Features are project-scoped capability/requirement units** – they must resolve to a parent project (if multiple projects exist) or remain unlinked
3. **Task assignment hierarchy:** Features can directly contain tasks (has_task), but projects can also contain tasks (belongs_to) for cross-feature work
4. **Semantic distinction:** Projects are named initiatives; features are capability descriptions with optional owner assignment
5. **Deduplication is scoped:** Projects deduplicate across the whole workspace; features deduplicate across all projects in the workspace
6. **Extraction signal:** Feature classification explicitly looks for capability/requirement language ("supports", "provides", "enables") and artifact names
7. **No explicit project classification rule** in the prompt—projects are inferred from named business context and are the top-level named entity in the hierarchy

This design reflects a **hierarchical work breakdown structure:** Workspace > Project > Feature > Task, with optional cross-cutting decision/question entities that can attach to projects or features at any level.

  "thought": "Now I have the full picture. Let me analyze the specific gaps:\n\n**1. No explicit project classification rule in the prompt**\nLine 30-36 defines \"Kind classification rules\" for: Decision, Task, Feature, Question. There is NO rule for Project. The model has to infer what a project is purely from examples (lines 67-69).\n\n**2. Feature catch-all is too broad**\nLine 36: \"Prioritization/scoping statements without explicit execution action are feature.\" - This means anything that isn't clearly a task, decision, or question defaults to feature. There's no path to classify something as a project unless it matches the pattern \"I'm building X\" or \"Project X needs...\"\n\n**3. The golden cases are very limited for project extraction**\nOnly 4 project test cases:\n- \"Our project is called Schack Systems\" → project (explicit naming)\n- \"Project Atlas needs an auth feature\" → project Atlas (explicit \"Project\" prefix)\n- \"I'm building Schack Systems\" → project (building language)\n- \"I'm building Schack Systems and shipping...\" → project + decision\n\nAll of these use very explicit naming. There are NO test cases for ambiguous situations like:\n- \"We're starting a mobile app\" (project or feature?)\n- \"Authentication is the next big thing we need\" (project or feature?)\n- \"The dashboard needs to be rebuilt from scratch\" (project or feature?)\n\n**4. The resolveEntityProject() heuristic is fragile**\nIn workspace-scope.ts, when multiple projects exist, it does a simple substring match of project names in the prompt text. This means:\n- Short project names will match too aggressively\n- Long project names may not appear in casual conversation\n- No semantic matching is used (despite embeddings being available)\n\n**Proposed improvements:**\n\nA. **Add explicit project classification rule to the prompt** - Add a \"Project:\" line in the Kind classification rules section with clear markers like: named product/system/initiative, building/launching/creating language, organizational scope containers.\n\nB. **Add distinguishing guidance** - Add a rule like: \"If a concept is a named product, system, or initiative that would contain multiple features, classify as project. If it's a specific capability or deliverable within a broader initiative, classify as feature.\"\n\nC. **Add more golden test cases** for:\n- Ambiguous project vs feature cases\n- Projects without the word \"project\" (\"We're launching a mobile app\")\n- Features that sound big (\"Authentication system\") in context of an existing project\n- The \"scale heuristic\" - things that contain other things are projects\n\nD. **Add an onboarding-specific hint** - During onboarding, the model should be more aggressive about extracting projects since the user is describing their business for the first time.\n\nE. **Improve resolveEntityProject()** - Consider using embedding similarity instead of just name substring matching.\n\nLet me now figure out what I want to ask the user before writing the plan.",

   154→
   155→export function resolveEntityProject(
   156→  entityText: string,
   157→  promptText: string,
   158→  projects: ProjectScopeRow[],
   159→): RecordId<"project", string> | undefined {
   160→  if (projects.length === 0) {
   161→    return undefined;
   162→  }
   163→
   164→  if (projects.length === 1) {
   165→    return projects[0].id;
   166→  }
   167→
   168→  const haystack = `${promptText}\n${entityText}`.toLowerCase();
   169→  const matchingProjects = projects.filter((project) => haystack.includes(project.name.toLowerCase()));
   170→
   171→  if (matchingProjects.length !== 1) {
   172→    return undefined;
   173→  }
   174→
   175→  return matchingProjects[0].id;
   176→}
   177→

{
  "subagent_type": "Plan",
  "prompt": "I need to plan improvements for the project vs feature classification in an LLM-based entity extraction system. Here's the full context:\n\n## Current System\n\n### Extraction Prompt (app/src/server/extraction/prompt.ts)\nThe prompt defines \"Kind classification rules\" (lines 30-36) for Decision, Task, Feature, and Question. **There is NO explicit classification rule for Project.** \n\nCurrent rules:\n- Decision: commitment/selection language (let's go with, move forward with, we decided...)\n- Task: concrete executable work with action verb (implement, build, fix...)\n- Feature: capability/requirement or scoped artifact description (supports, provides, enables; cards/dashboards/flows/screens/integrations)\n- Question: explicit question framing or question mark\n- \"Prioritization/scoping statements without explicit execution action are feature\" (catch-all)\n\nProjects are only taught through examples:\n- \"I'm building Schack Systems\" → project: Schack Systems\n- \"My project needs work\" → nothing (placeholder rejection)\n\n### Data Model (schema/surreal-schema.surql)\n- **Project**: name, status, description, embedding, timestamps. Linked via `workspace -> has_project -> project`\n- **Feature**: name, status, description, prd, owner, owner_name, embedding, timestamps. Linked via `project -> has_feature -> feature`\n- Hierarchy: Workspace → Project → Feature → Task\n\n### Feature-to-Project Resolution (app/src/server/workspace/workspace-scope.ts, resolveEntityProject)\nWhen a feature is extracted, it resolves to a parent project:\n1. 0 projects → unlinked\n2. 1 project → auto-assign\n3. Multiple projects → substring match of project name in prompt text, only assign if exactly 1 match\n\n### Golden Test Cases (evals/data/golden-cases.json)\nOnly 4 project-related cases, all using explicit naming (\"Schack Systems\", \"Atlas\"). No ambiguous cases.\n\n## Problems Identified\n\n1. **No explicit project classification rule** — the prompt tells the model what decisions, tasks, features, and questions are, but never defines what a project is. The model must infer this from 2 examples.\n\n2. **Feature catch-all absorbs projects** — \"Prioritization/scoping statements without explicit execution action are feature\" means ambiguous entities default to feature. \"We're launching a mobile app\" could reasonably be a project but may get classified as feature since it's a \"scoped artifact description.\"\n\n3. **No scale/scope guidance** — The prompt doesn't teach the model to consider hierarchical scope. \"Authentication\" is a feature within a project, but \"Mobile Banking App\" is a project that contains features. The model has no guidance on this distinction.\n\n4. **Weak project resolution heuristic** — resolveEntityProject() uses substring matching which is fragile for short project names (false positives) and doesn't leverage the embeddings already stored on entities.\n\n5. **Sparse project eval coverage** — Only 4 golden cases test project extraction, and all are unambiguous. No edge cases testing the project-vs-feature boundary.\n\n## Improvement Ideas\n\n### A. Prompt improvements (prompt.ts)\n- Add explicit \"Project:\" classification rule with markers: named product/system/initiative, building/launching/creating language, organizational scope that would contain features\n- Add disambiguation guidance: \"If something is a named product or system containing multiple capabilities, classify as project. If it's a specific capability within a larger initiative, classify as feature.\"\n- Add more examples showing the project-vs-feature boundary\n\n### B. Golden test cases (golden-cases.json)\n- Add ambiguous cases that test the boundary\n- Add cases where context (existing project) should influence classification\n- Add cases for projects without the word \"project\"\n\n### C. resolveEntityProject() improvements (workspace-scope.ts)\n- Consider using embedding similarity in addition to or instead of name substring matching\n- Add fuzzy/normalized matching\n\nPlease create a detailed, prioritized implementation plan. Focus on the highest-impact, lowest-risk changes first. The prompt and eval improvements are likely highest impact since they directly affect classification quality.",
  "description": "Plan project vs feature improvements"
}

Linked via `project -> has_feature -> feature`\n- Hierarchy: Workspace → Project → Feature → Task\n\n### Feature-to-Project Resolution (app/src/server/workspace/workspace-scope.ts, resolveEntityProject)\nWhen a feature is extracted, it resolves to a parent project:\n1. 0 projects → unlinked\n2. 1 project → auto-assign\n3. Multiple projects → substring match of project name in prompt text, only assign if exactly 1 match\n\n### Golden Test Cases (evals/data/golden-cases.json)\nOnly 4 project-related cases, all using explicit naming (\"Schack Systems\", \"Atlas\"). No ambiguous cases.\n\n## Problems Identified\n\n1. **No explicit project classification rule** — the prompt tells the model what decisions, tasks, features, and questions are, but never defines what a project is. The model must infer this from 2 examples.\n\n2. **Feature catch-all absorbs projects** — \"Prioritization/scoping statements without explicit execution action are feature\" means ambiguous entities default to feature. \"We're launching a mobile app\" could reasonably be a project but may get classified as feature since it's a \"scoped artifact description.\"\n\n3. **No scale/scope guidance** — The prompt doesn't teach the model to consider hierarchical scope. \"Authentication\" is a feature within a project, but \"Mobile Banking App\" is a project that contains features. The model has no guidance on this distinction.\n\n4. **Weak project resolution heuristic** — resolveEntityProject() uses substring matching which is fragile for short project names (false positives) and doesn't leverage the embeddings already stored on entities.\n\n5. **Sparse project eval coverage** — Only 4 golden cases test project extraction, and all are unambiguous. No edge cases testing the project-vs-feature boundary.\n\n## Improvement Ideas\n\n### A. Prompt improvements (prompt.ts)\n- Add explicit \"Project:\" classification rule with markers: named product/system/initiative, building/launching/creating language, organizational scope that would contain features\n- Add disambiguation guidance: \"If something is a named product or system containing multiple capabilities, classify as project. If it's a specific capability within a larger initiative, classify as feature.\"\n- Add more examples showing the project-vs-feature boundary\n\n### B. Golden test cases (golden-cases.json)\n- Add ambiguous cases that test the boundary\n- Add cases where context (existing project) should influence classification\n- Add cases for projects without the word \"project\"\n\n### C. resolveEntityProject() improvements (workspace-scope.ts)\n- Consider using embedding similarity in addition to or instead of name substring matching\n- Add fuzzy/normalized matching\n\nPlease create a detailed, prioritized implementation plan. Focus on the highest-impact, lowest-risk changes first. The prompt and eval improvements are likely highest impact since they directly affect classification quality.",

}

Here is the detailed, prioritized implementation plan.

---

## Implementation Plan: Project vs Feature Classification Improvements

### Summary of Findings

After thorough exploration of the codebase, I confirmed all five problems identified in the prompt and uncovered additional context that refines the plan.

**Key observations from the code:**

1. The prompt at `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/prompt.ts` lines 30-36 defines explicit classification rules for Decision, Task, Feature, and Question. Project is conspicuously absent. The model only learns "project" from the `z.enum` in the schema and two examples at lines 67 and 69.

2. The feature catch-all at line 36 ("Prioritization/scoping statements without explicit execution action are feature") is specifically designed to capture ambiguous scope-level statements. This directly competes with project classification since project descriptions are often scope/initiative statements without action verbs.

3. The `resolveEntityProject()` function at `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/workspace/workspace-scope.ts` lines 155-176 uses purely lowercase substring matching. Both projects and features already store embeddings (the schema defines HNSW indexes on both tables), but the resolution function never uses them.

4. The eval test kit's `seedWorkspace()` at `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/evals/eval-test-kit.ts` line 118 creates a default project named "Eval Project". This means every golden case runs with exactly 1 workspace project, which triggers the auto-assign path (line 164 of workspace-scope.ts) for every feature/task/decision/question. The multi-project resolution path (substring matching) is never exercised by evals.

5. No unit tests exist for `resolveEntityProject` -- only two files reference it, both in production code.

6. The entity matching in eval scorers at `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/evals/scorers/shared.ts` line 51 requires `expectedEntity.kind !== extractedEntity.kind` to fail fast. A project misclassified as feature (or vice versa) scores 0 on both precision and recall for that entity.

---

### Phase 1: Prompt Classification Rule for Project (Highest Impact, Lowest Risk)

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/prompt.ts`

**What to change:** Insert a Project classification rule into the "Kind classification rules:" block (after line 30, before or alongside the existing Decision/Task/Feature/Question rules). Also add a disambiguation heuristic and 2-3 boundary examples.

**Specific additions to the prompt string array:**

1. **Add a Project classification rule** (insert between lines 30-31, among the other kind rules):
   ```
   "- Project: named product, system, platform, or initiative encompassing multiple features (building, launching, creating, developing, starting, working on; named with a proper noun or product name)."
   ```

2. **Add disambiguation guidance** (insert after line 36, after the feature catch-all):
   ```
   "- Project vs feature disambiguation: if something is a named product or system that would contain multiple capabilities, classify as project. If it is a specific capability, module, or screen within a larger initiative, classify as feature. When unsure, ask: would this item have features underneath it? If yes, it is a project."
   ```

3. **Amend the feature catch-all** at line 36 to exclude project-scope items:
   Change from:
   ```
   "- Prioritization/scoping statements without explicit execution action are feature."
   ```
   To:
   ```
   "- Prioritization/scoping statements about a specific capability without explicit execution action are feature (not project-level initiatives)."
   ```

4. **Add boundary examples** (insert near lines 67-70 among existing examples):
   ```
   "- Input: We're launching a mobile banking app called FinVault. => project: FinVault.",
   "- Input: The app needs a push notification system. => feature: push notification system.",
   "- Input: We're building an e-commerce platform with product search and checkout. => project: e-commerce platform; feature: product search; feature: checkout.",
   "- Input: Authentication needs to support OAuth and SSO. => feature: Authentication (not project, because it is a capability within a larger system).",
   ```

**Why this is highest priority:** The prompt is the single point of control for what the LLM extracts. Without a project classification rule, the model must rely on two examples and its own reasoning. An explicit rule with disambiguation guidance directly addresses problems 1, 2, and 3 from the analysis.

**Risk mitigation:** The additions are purely additive to the existing prompt. Existing classification rules for Decision, Task, Feature, and Question are untouched. The only modification is the small amendment to the feature catch-all to add "(not project-level initiatives)", which narrows its scope slightly. Run the full eval suite after this change to verify no regressions on existing cases.

---

### Phase 3: Unit Tests for resolveEntityProject (Medium Impact, Zero Risk)

**New file:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/tests/unit/resolve-entity-project.test.ts`

**What to test:** The pure function `resolveEntityProject` from workspace-scope.ts has no unit tests. Add deterministic tests covering:

1. **Zero projects** -- returns undefined
2. **Single project** -- auto-assigns regardless of text content
3. **Multiple projects, one name matches** -- returns the matching project
4. **Multiple projects, no name matches** -- returns undefined
5. **Multiple projects, multiple names match** -- returns undefined (ambiguous)
6. **Short project name false positive** -- e.g., project named "App" matching "We need an app notification feature" (demonstrates the weakness)
7. **Case-insensitive matching** -- "atlas" matches project "Atlas"

These tests use `RecordId` objects from `surrealdb` and the `ProjectScopeRow` type. They are pure unit tests with no database dependency, following the pattern of existing tests like `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/tests/unit/dedup.test.ts`.

**Why this matters before Phase 4:** Before changing the `resolveEntityProject` logic, you need regression tests to ensure the current behavior is captured and any improvements do not break the auto-assign and single-match paths.

---

### Phase 4: Improve resolveEntityProject with Normalized Matching (Medium Impact, Medium Risk)

**File:** `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/workspace/workspace-scope.ts`

**Current behavior (lines 155-176):**
```typescript
export function resolveEntityProject(
  entityText: string,
  promptText: string,
  projects: ProjectScopeRow[],
): RecordId<"project", string> | undefined {
  if (projects.length === 0) return undefined;
  if (projects.length === 1) return projects[0].id;
  
  const haystack = `${promptText}\n${entityText}`.toLowerCase();
  const matchingProjects = projects.filter((project) => 
    haystack.includes(project.name.toLowerCase())
  );
  if (matchingProjects.length !== 1) return undefined;
  return matchingProjects[0].id;
}
```

**Proposed changes:**

1. **Add word-boundary-aware matching** to prevent short project names from false-positive matching. Replace the raw `haystack.includes(project.name.toLowerCase())` with a regex that checks for word boundaries:
   ```typescript
   function projectNameMatchesInText(projectName: string, text: string): boolean {
     const normalizedName = projectName.toLowerCase().trim();
     if (normalizedName.length === 0) return false;
     const escaped = normalizedName.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
     const pattern = new RegExp(`\\b${escaped}\\b`, 'i');
     return pattern.test(text);
   }
   ```

2. **Keep the function signature unchanged** -- it remains a pure function taking the same inputs and returning the same output type. The change is only in the matching predicate.

3. **Do NOT add embedding similarity yet.** The function signature is `(entityText, promptText, projects: ProjectScopeRow[])` where `ProjectScopeRow` only has `id` and `name`. Adding embedding similarity would require:
   - Changing the type to include `embedding?: number[]`
   - Making the function async
   - Generating an embedding for the entity text
   - Changing all call sites
   
   This is a larger change that should be deferred to a future phase when there is data showing that word-boundary matching is insufficient. The current pipeline already uses embedding similarity for dedup in `entity-upsert.ts` (cosine > 0.95 + fuzzy name match), so embedding-based project resolution would be architecturally consistent but is not the highest priority.

**Risk:** Medium. The word-boundary change could cause regressions if project names contain characters that are not word-boundary-safe. The unit tests from Phase 3 protect against this. The single-project auto-assign path (which covers most real usage) is unaffected.

---

### Phase 5 (Optional, Future): Graph Context Awareness for Scope Disambiguation

**Files:** 
- `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/extract-graph.ts` (formatExtractionGraphContext)
- `/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain/zagreb-v3/app/src/server/extraction/prompt.ts`

**Concept:** The extraction model already receives "Existing graph context" showing previously extracted entities with their kinds (line 44 of extract-graph.ts). The format at line 98 is:
```
[entity:feature:uuid] feature: push notifications (confidence 0.92, source message uuid)
```

If the model sees existing projects in the graph context, it could use that signal to disambiguate. For example, if the graph already has `[entity:project:uuid] project: FinVault`, then "we need a payment system" would be classified as feature (it's a capability within the existing project) rather than project.

**What to add to the prompt:**
```
"- When existing graph context shows a project, new capabilities mentioned in the same conversation are likely features of that project, not separate projects."
```

**Why this is lower priority:** The graph context is already fed to the model. A well-tuned prompt (Phase 1) should implicitly handle this. Adding an explicit rule only makes sense if eval results show the model is creating duplicate projects or misclassifying features-of-existing-projects.

---

- New project boundary cases: do they pass?
- Existing project cases (`project-basic`, `multi-entity`, `placeholder-allowed-real-name`, `project-and-release-commitment`): no regressions?
- Existing feature cases (`feature-basic`, `grounded-evidence`): no regressions?
- The catch-all feature case `grounded-evidence` ("onboarding summary card") is particularly important -- it should remain classified as feature, not project.

if TradeSync is a project, then those would be sub projects, or?12:21 AMYou're right. It depends on context the extraction pipeline already has — the workspace name and existing projects.
If TradeSync is the workspace: "Order routing, risk management, and compliance" are three distinct workstreams → 3 Projects.
If TradeSync is a project: Those are either 3 Features (if they're capabilities being built) or just a description (if the user is explaining what it does). But they're never sub-projects — the data model doesn't have sub-projects. Projects are flat under Workspace.
This is exactly what the extraction prompt's context injection is for. The pipeline already receives existing entities (workspace name, current projects) as context. The LLM sees "workspace is TradeSync" and knows these are project-level. Or it sees "project TradeSync exists" and knows these are feature-level or descriptive.
No schema change needed — the LLM just needs the context it already gets to make the right scoping call.

     8→import { elapsedMs, logError, logInfo } from "../http/observability";
     9→
    10→export async function extractStructuredGraph(input: {
    11→  extractionModel: any;
    12→  conversationHistory: MessageContextRow[];
    13→  currentMessage?: MessageContextRow;
    14→  graphContext: ExtractionGraphContextRow[];
    15→  sourceText: string;
    16→  onboarding: boolean;
    17→  heading?: string;
    18→}): Promise<ExtractionPromptOutput> {
    19→  const startedAt = performance.now();
    20→  logInfo("extraction.generate.started", "Structured extraction started", {
    21→    onboarding: input.onboarding,
    22→    hasHeading: input.heading !== undefined,
    23→    contextMessageCount: input.conversationHistory.length,
    24→    hasCurrentMessage: input.currentMessage !== undefined,
    25→    graphContextCount: input.graphContext.length,
    26→    sourceLength: input.sourceText.length,
    27→  });
    28→
    29→  try {
    30→    const extractionOutput = await generateObject({
    31→      model: input.extractionModel,
    32→      schema: extractionResultSchema,
    33→      system: buildExtractionSystemPrompt({ onboarding: input.onboarding }),
    34→      prompt: [
    35→        "Conversation history (reference resolution only):",
    36→        formatExtractionConversationHistory(input.conversationHistory),
    37→        "",
    38→        "Current message (extract only from this text when message metadata exists):",
    39→        formatExtractionCurrentMessage(input.currentMessage, input.sourceText),
    40→        "",
    41→        "Resolved-from lineage constraints:",
    42→        formatResolvedFromConstraints(input.currentMessage, input.conversationHistory),
    43→        "",
    44→        "Existing graph context (semantic index of prior extracted entities):",
    45→        formatExtractionGraphContext(input.graphContext),
    46→        input.heading ? `Section heading: ${input.heading}` : "",
    47→        "",
    48→        "Current source text:",
    49→        input.sourceText,
    50→      ]
    51→        .filter((line) => line.length > 0)
    52→        .join("\n"),
    53→    });
    54→
    55→    const output = extractionOutput.object as ExtractionPromptOutput;
    56→    logInfo("extraction.generate.completed", "Structured extraction completed", {
    57→      onboarding: input.onboarding,
    58→      entityCount: output.entities.length,
    59→      relationshipCount: output.relationships.length,
    60→      toolCount: output.tools.length,
    61→      durationMs: elapsedMs(startedAt),
    62→    });
    63→
    64→    return output;
    65→  } catch (error) {
    66→    logError("extraction.generate.failed", "Structured extraction failed", error, {
    67→      onboarding: input.onboarding,
    68→      durationMs: elapsedMs(startedAt),
    69→    });
    70→    throw error;
    71→  }
    72→}
    73→
    74→function formatExtractionConversationHistory(rows: MessageContextRow[]): string {
    75→  if (rows.length === 0) {
    76→    return "(no prior messages)";
    77→  }
    78→
    79→  return rows.map((row) => `[message:${row.id.id as string}] ${row.role.toUpperCase()}: ${row.text}`).join("\n");
    80→}
    81→
    82→function formatExtractionCurrentMessage(currentMessage: MessageContextRow | undefined, sourceText: string): string {
    83→  if (!currentMessage) {
    84→    return `(no message metadata; source text: ${sourceText})`;
    85→  }
    86→
    87→  return `[message:${currentMessage.id.id as string}] ${currentMessage.role.toUpperCase()}: ${currentMessage.text}`;
    88→}
    89→
    90→function formatExtractionGraphContext(rows: ExtractionGraphContextRow[]): string {
    91→  if (rows.length === 0) {
    92→    return "(no prior extracted entities)";
    93→  }
    94→
    95→  return rows
    96→    .map((row) => {
    97→      const table = row.id.tb;
    98→      return `[entity:${table}:${row.id.id as string}] ${row.kind}: ${row.text} (confidence ${row.confidence.toFixed(2)}, source message ${row.sourceMessage.id as string})`;
    99→    })
   100→    .join("\n");
   101→}
   102→
   103→function formatResolvedFromConstraints(
   104→  currentMessage: MessageContextRow | undefined,
   105→  conversationHistory: MessageContextRow[],
   106→): string {
   107→  if (!currentMessage) {
   108→    return "Current message id unavailable; omit resolvedFromMessageId.";
   109→  }
   110→
   111→  const currentMessageId = currentMessage.id.id as string;
   112→  if (conversationHistory.length === 0) {
   113→    return [
   114→      `Current message id (forbidden for resolvedFromMessageId): ${currentMessageId}`,
   115→      "Allowed resolvedFromMessageId values (history only): none",
   116→      "Omit resolvedFromMessageId.",
   117→    ].join("\n");
   118→  }
   119→
   120→  const historyMessageIds = conversationHistory.map((row) => row.id.id as string);
   121→  return [
   122→    `Current message id (forbidden for resolvedFromMessageId): ${currentMessageId}`,
   123→    `Allowed resolvedFromMessageId values (history only): ${historyMessageIds.join(", ")}`,
   124→    "Use one of the allowed ids exactly, or omit resolvedFromMessageId.",
   125→  ].join("\n");
   126→}
   127→
```

     1→import { RecordId, type Surreal } from "surrealdb";
     2→import { readEntityText } from "./entity-text";
     3→import type {
     4→  ConversationProvenanceRow,
     5→  ExtractionConversationContext,
     6→  ExtractionGraphContextRow,
     7→  MessageContextRow,
     8→} from "./types";
     9→
    10→type ExtractableEntityKind = "project" | "person" | "feature" | "task" | "decision" | "question";
    11→
    12→export async function loadAssistantConversationContext(
    13→  surreal: Surreal,
    14→  conversationId: string,
    15→): Promise<MessageContextRow[]> {
    16→  const conversationRecord = new RecordId("conversation", conversationId);
    17→  const [rows] = await surreal
    18→    .query<[MessageContextRow[]]>(
    19→      "RETURN fn::conversation_recent($conversation, $limit);",
    20→      {
    21→        conversation: conversationRecord,
    22→        limit: 10,
    23→      },
    24→    )
    25→    .collect<[MessageContextRow[]]>();
    26→
    27→  return [...rows].reverse();
    28→}
    29→
    30→export async function loadExtractionConversationContext(input: {
    31→  surreal: Surreal;
    32→  conversationId: string;
    33→  currentMessageRecord: RecordId<"message", string>;
    34→}): Promise<ExtractionConversationContext> {
    35→  const conversationRecord = new RecordId("conversation", input.conversationId);
    36→  const [rows] = await input.surreal
    37→    .query<[MessageContextRow[]]>(
    38→      "RETURN fn::conversation_recent($conversation, $limit);",
    39→      {
    40→        conversation: conversationRecord,
    41→        limit: 31,
    42→      },
    43→    )
    44→    .collect<[MessageContextRow[]]>();
    45→
    46→  const ordered = [...rows].reverse();
    47→  const currentMessage = ordered.find((row) => row.id.id === input.currentMessageRecord.id);
    48→  if (!currentMessage) {
    49→    throw new Error(`current message not found in conversation context: ${input.currentMessageRecord.id as string}`);
    50→  }
    51→
    52→  return {
    53→    conversationHistory: ordered.filter((row) => row.id.id !== input.currentMessageRecord.id).slice(-30),
    54→    currentMessage,
    55→  };
    56→}
    57→
    58→export async function loadConversationGraphContext(
    59→  surreal: Surreal,
    60→  conversationId: string,
    61→  limit: number,
    62→): Promise<ExtractionGraphContextRow[]> {
    63→  const conversationRecord = new RecordId("conversation", conversationId);
    64→  const [rows] = await surreal
    65→    .query<[ConversationProvenanceRow[]]>(
    66→      [
    67→        "SELECT `in`, out, confidence, extracted_at",
    68→        "FROM extraction_relation",
    69→        "WHERE `in` IN (SELECT VALUE id FROM message WHERE conversation = $conversation)",
    70→        "ORDER BY extracted_at DESC",
    71→        "LIMIT $limit;",
    72→      ].join(" "),
    73→      { conversation: conversationRecord, limit },
    74→    )
    75→    .collect<[ConversationProvenanceRow[]]>();
    76→
    77→  const seen = new Set<string>();
    78→  const items: ExtractionGraphContextRow[] = [];
    79→
    80→  for (const row of rows) {
    81→    const entityId = row.out.id as string;
    82→    if (seen.has(entityId)) {
    83→      continue;
    84→    }
    85→
    86→    const entityTable = row.out.tb;
    87→    if (
    88→      entityTable !== "project" &&
    89→      entityTable !== "person" &&
    90→      entityTable !== "feature" &&
    91→      entityTable !== "task" &&
    92→      entityTable !== "decision" &&
    93→      entityTable !== "question"
    94→    ) {
    95→      continue;
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    const entityText = await readEntityText(surreal, row.out);
    99→    if (!entityText) {
   100→      continue;
   101→    }
   102→
   103→    items.push({
   104→      id: row.out,
   105→      kind: entityTable as ExtractableEntityKind,
   106→      text: entityText,
   107→      confidence: row.confidence,
   108→      sourceMessage: row.in,
   109→    });
   110→    seen.add(entityId);
   111→  }
   112→
   113→  return items;
   114→}
   115→
```

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Improve Project vs Feature Classification

## Context

The extraction prompt defines explicit classification rules for Decision, Task, Feature, and Question — but **not for Project**. The model learns "project" from only 2 examples. Meanwhile, the feature catch-all ("Prioritization/scoping statements without explicit execution action are feature") absorbs ambiguous entities that could be projects.

The project-vs-feature distinction is **contextual, not purely linguistic**. The same entity ("order routing") is a project if the workspace is "TradeSync", or a feature if "TradeSync" is already a project. The data model is flat: `Workspace → Project → Feature → Task` with no sub-projects.

### Key findings
- `prompt.ts:30-36` — Kind classification rules exist for 4/5 kinds (project is missing)
- `prompt.ts:36` — Feature catch-all absorbs project-scope statements
- **Workspace name is never injected into the extraction prompt** — the model sees graph context entities but not the workspace identity. This means it can't use workspace scope to disambiguate.
- `extract-graph.ts:34-50` — Prompt includes conversation history, current message, graph context, and source text. No workspace metadata.
- `golden-cases.json` — Only 4 project test cases, all unambiguous
- `workspace-scope.ts:155-176` — `resolveEntityProject()` uses raw substring matching, fragile for short names
- Eval seed creates workspace named `Eval ${timestamp}` and 1 project named "Eval Project", so multi-project resolution is never exercised

## Changes

### Phase 1: Inject workspace context into extraction prompt
**Files:** `app/src/server/extraction/extract-graph.ts`, `app/src/server/chat/chat-processor.ts`

The extraction model needs to know the workspace name and existing project names to make correct scoping decisions. Add a `workspaceContext` section to the prompt:

1. Extend `extractStructuredGraph()` input to accept `workspaceName: string` and `projectNames: string[]`
2. Add a new prompt section between "Existing graph context" and "Current source text":
   ```
   Workspace scope:
   Workspace: "TradeSync"
   Existing projects: "Order Routing", "Compliance"
   ```
3. Pass workspace name and project names from the chat processor (both are already loaded in the processing pipeline via `input.workspace.name` and `loadWorkspaceProjects()`)
4. Do the same for `ingestAttachment()` in `document-ingestion.ts` which also calls `extractStructuredGraph()`

### Phase 2: Add project classification rule + scope guidance to prompt
**File:** `app/src/server/extraction/prompt.ts`

1. Add explicit Project rule in the "Kind classification rules:" block:
   ```
   - Project: named product, system, or initiative that is a top-level workstream within the workspace
     (building, launching, creating, developing; identified by a proper noun or product name).
   ```

2. Add scope-aware disambiguation guidance after the feature catch-all:
   ```
   - Project vs feature scope rule: entities at the top level of a workspace are projects.
     Capabilities within an existing project are features. Use the Workspace scope section
     to determine hierarchy. If the workspace itself is the product, its major workstreams
     are projects. If projects already exist, new capabilities described within them are features.
   ```

3. Narrow the feature catch-all from:
   ```
   Prioritization/scoping statements without explicit execution action are feature.
   ```
   To:
   ```
   Prioritization/scoping statements about a specific capability without explicit execution action are feature (not project-level initiatives).
   ```

4. Add boundary examples:
   - "We're launching a mobile banking app called FinVault." → project: FinVault
   - "The app needs a push notification system." → feature: push notification system
   - "Authentication needs to support OAuth and SSO." → feature: Authentication (capability, not top-level workstream)

### Phase 3: Add golden test cases for project-feature boundary
**File:** `evals/data/golden-cases.json`

Add ~7 new cases covering:
- Projects without the word "project" (named initiative with building language)
- Features that sound big but aren't projects (authentication module, analytics dashboard)
- Feature within existing project context (using `workspace_seed`)
- Named system that's clearly a project ("We're developing Meridian")

### Phase 4: Unit tests for resolveEntityProject
**New file:** `tests/unit/resolve-entity-project.test.ts`

Test the pure function before modifying it:
- 0 projects → undefined
- 1 project → auto-assign
- Multiple projects, one match → correct project
- Multiple projects, no match → undefined
- Multiple projects, ambiguous → undefined
- Short name false positive ("App" matching in arbitrary text)

### Phase 5: Word-boundary matching in resolveEntityProject
**File:** `app/src/server/workspace/workspace-scope.ts`

Replace raw `haystack.includes(name)` with word-boundary-aware regex matching. Prevents short project names (e.g., "App") from false-positive matching inside unrelated words. Keeps the function signature unchanged (pure, synchronous).

## Verification

1. Run baseline evals before changes: `bun run eval`
2. After Phases 1-3, clear eval cache and re-run
3. Check: new boundary cases pass, existing project/feature cases don't regress
4. Key regression targets: `feature-basic`, `grounded-evidence` (must stay feature), `project-basic`, `multi-entity` (must stay project)
5. After Phase 4+5, run `bun test tests/unit/resolve-entity-project.test.ts`

## Files touched
- `app/src/server/extraction/extract-graph.ts` — workspace context injection
- `app/src/server/extraction/prompt.ts` — classification rules + scope guidance
- `app/src/server/chat/chat-processor.ts` — pass workspace data to extraction
- `app/src/server/extraction/document-ingestion.ts` — pass workspace data for attachment extraction
- `evals/data/golden-cases.json` — test coverage
- `tests/unit/resolve-entity-project.test.ts` — new unit test file
- `app/src/server/workspace/workspace-scope.ts` — matching logic

```

    18→export async function processChatMessage(input: {
    19→  deps: ServerDependencies;
    20→  conversationId: string;
    21→  messageId: string;
    22→  workspaceRecord: RecordId<"workspace", string>;
    23→  userMessageRecord: RecordId<"message", string>;
    24→  userText: string;
    25→  attachment?: IncomingAttachment;
    26→  onboardingAction?: OnboardingAction;
    27→}): Promise<void> {
    28→  const startedAt = performance.now();
    29→  logInfo("chat.message.process.execution.started", "Chat message processing execution started", {
    30→    conversationId: input.conversationId,
    31→    messageId: input.messageId,
    32→    workspaceId: input.workspaceRecord.id as string,
    33→    hasAttachment: input.attachment !== undefined,
    34→  });
    35→
    36→  try {
    37→    const now = new Date();
    38→    const conversationRecord = new RecordId("conversation", input.conversationId);
    39→    const workspace = await input.deps.surreal.select<WorkspaceRow>(input.workspaceRecord);
    40→    if (!workspace) {
    41→      throw new Error("workspace not found");
    42→    }
    43→
    44→    const conversation = await input.deps.surreal.select<ConversationRow>(conversationRecord);
    45→    if (!conversation) {
    46→      throw new Error("conversation not found");
    47→    }
    48→
    49→    const assistantContextRows = await loadAssistantConversationContext(input.deps.surreal, input.conversationId);
    50→    const extractionConversationContext = await loadExtractionConversationContext({
    51→      surreal: input.deps.surreal,
    52→      conversationId: input.conversationId,
    53→      currentMessageRecord: input.userMessageRecord,
    54→    });
    55→    const extractionGraphContext = await loadConversationGraphContext(input.deps.surreal, input.conversationId, 60);
    56→    const persistedEntities: ExtractedEntity[] = [];
    57→    const persistedRelationships: ExtractedRelationship[] = [];
    58→    const seedItems: OnboardingSeedItem[] = [];
    59→    const embeddingTargets: Array<{ record: GraphEntityRecord; text: string }> = [];
    60→    const extractedTools: string[] = [];
    61→    const unresolvedAssigneeNames = new Set<string>();
    62→
    63→    if (input.attachment) {
    64→      const ingestion = await ingestAttachment({
    65→        surreal: input.deps.surreal,
    66→        extractionModel: input.deps.extractionModel,
    67→        embeddingModel: input.deps.embeddingModel,
    68→        embeddingDimension: input.deps.config.embeddingDimension,
    69→        extractionStoreThreshold: input.deps.config.extractionStoreThreshold,
    70→        extractionModelId: input.deps.config.extractionModelId,
    71→        workspaceRecord: input.workspaceRecord,
    72→        conversationRecord,
    73→        userMessageRecord: input.userMessageRecord,
    74→        attachment: input.attachment,
    75→        now,
    76→      });
    77→
    78→      persistedEntities.push(...ingestion.entities);
    79→      persistedRelationships.push(...ingestion.relationships);
    80→      seedItems.push(...ingestion.seeds);
    81→      embeddingTargets.push(...ingestion.embeddingTargets);
    82→      extractedTools.push(...ingestion.tools);
    83→      for (const unresolvedName of ingestion.unresolvedAssigneeNames) {
    84→        unresolvedAssigneeNames.add(unresolvedName);
    85→      }
    86→    }
    87→
    88→    const textExtraction = await extractStructuredGraph({
    89→      extractionModel: input.deps.extractionModel,
    90→      conversationHistory: extractionConversationContext.conversationHistory,
    91→      currentMessage: extractionConversationContext.currentMessage,
    92→      graphContext: extractionGraphContext,
    93→      sourceText: input.userText,
    94→      onboarding: !workspace.onboarding_complete,
    95→    });
    96→
    97→    const textPersistence = await persistExtractionOutput({
    98→      surreal: input.deps.surreal,
    99→      embeddingModel: input.deps.embeddingModel,
   100→      embeddingDimension: input.deps.config.embeddingDimension,
   101→      extractionModelId: input.deps.config.extractionModelId,
   102→      extractionStoreThreshold: input.deps.config.extractionStoreThreshold,
   103→      workspaceRecord: input.workspaceRecord,
   104→      sourceRecord: input.userMessageRecord as SourceRecord,
   105→      sourceKind: "message",

   111→      now,
   112→    });
   113→
   114→    persistedEntities.push(...textPersistence.entities);
   115→    persistedRelationships.push(...textPersistence.relationships);
   116→    seedItems.push(...textPersistence.seeds);
   117→    embeddingTargets.push(...textPersistence.embeddingTargets);
   118→    extractedTools.push(...textPersistence.tools);
   119→    for (const unresolvedName of textPersistence.unresolvedAssigneeNames) {
